$W_B(W^+) = 3$ and $W_B(W^-) = 4$ are the unique $\mathbb{Z}_7$ solutions consistent with
both doublets simultaneously (\lean{wb\_wplus\_uniquely\_determined},
\lean{wb\_wminus\_uniquely\_determined}, $\CatAL$).
\end{remark}

\begin{lemma}[W$^+$ Decay, $\CatAL$]
\label{lem:wplus_decay}
For all $(l, r) \in \mathbb{Z}_7^2$: $f_{\rm MDL}(l,\,3,\,r) = 0$.
A W$^+$ center cell (winding~3) always maps to vacuum in the next CA step.
Combined with $f_{\rm MDL}(2,0,2) = 3$ (Lemma~\ref{rem:sm_vertex}), the W$^+$ lifetime in the $f_{\rm MDL}$ CA is exactly 1 timestep: it is created by the $(u,\gamma,u)$ vertex event and decays immediately to vacuum.
This is the CA-level mass gap for the charged-current sector: the W$^+$ functions as a contact-interaction vertex mediator, not a propagating beable, consistent with the Fermi effective-theory picture at energies below $M_W$.
Lean-certified: \lean{wplus\_center\_maps\_to\_vacuum}, \lean{wplus\_creation\_then\_decay}
(\lean{GUTStructure.lean}~\S39, \lean{decide}, zero \texttt{sorry}).
See also~\cite{SpivackDeeperConsequences} for the full W$^+$/W$^-$ producibility asymmetry analysis.
\end{lemma}

At the cellular automaton (UV/sub-Fermi) level, the $W^+$ exchange between
fermions is a \emph{contact interaction} in the exact Fermi sense: there is no
relay mechanism.
An exhaustive search over $7^4 + 5^6 = 18{,}026$ initial seeds finds zero
configurations in which a $W^+$ produced at one vertex propagates to a second
vertex to mediate an interaction (Lean-certified, Lemma~\ref{lem:wplus_decay}, zero \texttt{sorry}).
The structural reason is the $W^+$ decay theorem (Lemma~\ref{lem:wplus_decay}):
$f_{\rm MDL}(l,3,r) = 0$ for all $l,r \in \mathbb{Z}_7$
(Lean-certified, zero \texttt{sorry}), meaning a $W^+$ center cell always
outputs vacuum in the next step; flanking $u$-quarks produced by the outgoing
$d$-quarks also decay immediately, leaving no residue for a subsequent vertex.
The CA $W^+$ exchange is therefore an exact analog of the Fermi effective theory,
valid at the fundamental (UV) scale where the CA operates.\footnote{\label{fn:planck_working_hypothesis}The precise
identification of the CA cell spacing with the Planck length is an open problem in
the GTE framework --- the relationship $a \approx \ell_{\rm Planck}$ is a working
hypothesis (following~\cite{tHooft2016CA}) rather than a GTE derivation; see P36
(open problem) and the discussion of the GTE gravitational coupling constant.}

\begin{remark}[CA-level mass gap duality, $\CatA$]
\label{rem:mass_gap_duality}
The $\mathbb{Z}_7 = 0$ photon is a stable CA beable: it corresponds to the
period-14 Rule~110 ether background, which propagates indefinitely at speed
$v_{\rm CA} = 2/3$ (computationally verified).
The $\mathbb{Z}_7 = 3$ $W^+$ boson is a contact vertex: it does not propagate
as a CA beable (null result from the relay search, computationally verified).
This dichotomy --- massless particle $\leftrightarrow$ stable CA propagation;
massive particle $\leftrightarrow$ CA contact vertex --- is the CA-arithmetic
signature of the mass gap.
The massive-particle contact picture recovers the Fermi effective theory
in the limit $q^2 \ll M_W^2$.
\end{remark}

The $W^+$ propagating picture (finite speed $v < c$, finite lifetime,
Breit-Wigner resonance) emerges at the level of the QFT Hilbert space, not the
CA beable level.
In the QFT Hilbert space built from CA beable superpositions (following the
't~Hooft cogwheel construction), the two-point function of the $W^+$ operator
receives Dyson-resummed self-energy corrections from the CA Dirac propagators,
producing a dynamical pole at $q^2 = M_W^2$ with $M_W/M_Z = \sqrt{10/13}$
(P31~\cite{SpivackWeinbergAngle}).
In the contact limit $q^2 \ll M_W^2$, the propagator reduces to $-1/M_W^2$,
recovering the CA contact vertex --- a self-consistency check of the
CA$\to$QFT bridge (computationally confirmed).

\begin{remark}[P22 charged-current absorption and $f_{\rm MDL}$ transparency, $\CatA$]
\label{rem:p22_transparency}
The charged-current absorption vertices of P22 correspond to transparency in
$f_{\rm MDL}$:
$f_{\rm MDL}(6,3,2) = f_{\rm MDL}(4,3,0) = f_{\rm MDL}(2,4,6) = f_{\rm MDL}(0,4,4) = 0$.
The two formalisms are complementary:
$f_{\rm MDL}$ encodes the $W^+$ emission amplitude --- the neighborhood
$(2,0,2) \to 3$ (Lean-certified: \lean{fmdl\_w\_plus\_unique\_neighborhood}) ---
while P22 encodes the $W^+$ exchange amplitude in the time-reversal dual channel
($d + W^+ \to u$).
CA transparency at P22's three-point absorption vertices reflects the absence of
vertex-level energy injection at the CA layer; the interaction amplitude is carried
by the P22 cross-field.
The two frameworks encode complementary dual aspects of the same physical vertex.

Of the 14 non-zero $f_{\rm MDL}$ neighborhoods, exactly 6 correspond to P22-admissible
effective bosons: 1 charged-current ($W^+$-emission, $(2,0,2)\!\to\!3$) and 5 neutral-current
transparency events where the center particle passes without scattering
($f_{\rm MDL}(l,c,r) = c$, $c \in \{0,1,2,5\}$).
The remaining 8 neighborhoods encode orbital transitions with effective boson winding
$W_B \in \{+1,-4\}$ that lie outside P22's renormalizable gauge spectrum ---
these are sub-vertex orbit dynamics not captured by phenomenological Feynman rules
(computationally verified, \texttt{ca\_vertex\_table.py}).
Furthermore, the charged-current vertex $f_{\rm MDL}(2,0,2) = 3$ provides the CA-arithmetic
certificate of P22's Silver closure theorem: $W^+$-mediated transitions appear with
exactly one CA neighborhood and effective boson winding $W_B = 3$.
P22~\cite{Spivack2026_UGPDynamics} derives the same charged-current structure
topologically from braid cobordism winding conservation; the CA neighborhood count
and the topological winding invariant are in precise correspondence.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[$F_{21} = \mathbb{Z}_7 \rtimes \mathbb{Z}_3$ color extension of $f_{\rm MDL}$, $\CatA$]
\label{rem:z3_color_extension}
The unique MDL-minimal extension on the $F_{21}$ substrate
$f_{\rm MDL,color} : (\mathbb{Z}_3 \times \mathbb{Z}_7)^3 \to \mathbb{Z}_3 \times \mathbb{Z}_7$
exists and is $\mathbb{Z}_3$-equivariant (color gauge invariant).
It encodes the SM orbit for each color class and has $34$ non-zero outputs
compared to $14$ for $f_{\rm MDL}$ (factor ${\times}2.43$).
Colored particles (quarks, gluons) are structurally separated from colorless
particles (leptons, EW bosons) at the CA level.
Color confinement analogue: isolated colored generation states cascade to vacuum
in exactly $N_{\rm gen} = 3$ steps
(computationally verified, \texttt{z3\_z7\_color\_extension.py}).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Color charge is canonically forced by GF(7), $\CatAL$]
\label{rem:color_sylow3}
The identification of $\{1,2,4\} \subset \mathbb{Z}_7^*$ as the color subgroup is not arbitrary.
$\mathbb{Z}_7^* \cong \mathbb{Z}_6$ (the multiplicative group of GF(7) is cyclic of order $6 = 2 \times 3$).
Its unique subgroup of order~3 is $\{1,2,4\}$, which is the Sylow-3 subgroup of $\mathbb{Z}_6$
and simultaneously the set of cubic roots of unity in GF(7) ($x^3 \equiv 1 \pmod{7}$).
There is no other $\mathbb{Z}_3 \hookrightarrow \mathbb{Z}_7^*$ — the embedding of three-color charge
into the $\mathbb{Z}_7$ structure is canonical, not chosen.
Machine-certified: \lean{color\_subgroup\_is\_sylow3} (GUTStructure.lean, zero \texttt{sorry}).
\end{remark}

\subsection{Massive Fermions from Beable Superposition}
\label{sec:massive_fermions_beable}

In the two-layer $\{$Rule~110, Rule~124$\}$ chiral CA, the massive Dirac fermion states
emerge from quantum superpositions of the two beable species $\{G_R, G_L\}$.
The quantum Hamiltonian in the beable basis is
\begin{equation}
H = vk\sigma_z + m_{\rm eff}(g)\,\sigma_x \quad (\text{computationally confirmed}),
\end{equation}
where $v = 2/3$ is the CA propagation speed (computationally verified) and
$m_{\rm eff}(g) \propto N_{\rm eff}(\mathrm{gen})$ plays the role of the Yukawa coupling
(arithmetic mass hierarchy, machine-certified in Lean~4).
The rest-frame fermion state
$|\mathrm{fermion},\mathrm{rest}\rangle = (1/\sqrt{2})(|G_R,w\rangle + |G_L,w\rangle)$
is forced by the symmetric light cone $|v_R| = |v_L| = 2/3$ (computationally verified).
Diagonalizing $H$ gives the helicity angle $\tan\theta_k = m_{\rm eff}/(vk)$.
No cell-level coupling between the two layers is invoked --- the mass arises from
quantum superposition at the Hilbert-space level, consistent with the coupling
uniqueness theorem (\S\ref{sec:cup3d_coupling}) (computationally confirmed).

% ── 10. Dark sector arithmetic hint ──────────────────────────────────────────
\section{Dark Sector Arithmetic}
\label{sec:dark}

The mirror-dual branch of the GTE (defined by swapping $b_2 = 42 \leftrightarrow q_2 = 24$)
gives a dark sector cascade with seed $c_1 = 2137$ (prime; $2137 \bmod 73 = 20$;
machine-verified). The dark sector generation orbit under $f_{\rm MDL}$ occupies the
mirror branch of the $\mathbb{Z}_7$ rule table, disjoint from the SM orbit.
As established in \S\ref{sec:introduction}, the two-level winding structure
$W_A$ (orbit-sum beable charge) and $W_B$ (SM interaction winding) applies throughout;
for the dark-sector orbit, the analogous distinction governs the dark-charge assignments below.

The dark-sector winding classes $\{w=1,\, w=5\}$ carry electric charges
$Q(\bar{d}) = +1/3$ and $Q(\bar{u}) = -2/3$ respectively (computationally verified, derived from
portal charge conservation: $Q_{\rm dark} = -Q_{\rm SM\,partner}$ for the vacuum
portal vertices $(0 \to 1+6)$ and $(0 \to 2+5)$;
\texttt{dark\_sector\_charge.py}).
These coincide with the anti-down and anti-up quarks of the SM.
The $\mathbb{Z}_7$ winding class $w=3$ appearing in the dark composite
$\bar{d}\bar{d}\bar{d}$ is a winding number, not an electric charge; the
composite's charge is $Q = 3\times(+1/3) = +1$ (anti-$\Delta^+$), consistent
with its identification as the anti-baryon sector~\cite{SpivackMirrorBraidAtlas}.
Note that this $\mathbb{Z}_7$-winding dark sector (windings $\{1,5\}$, identified as SM anti-quarks with $Q \neq 0$) is distinct from the mirror-branch dark sector of the companion paper~\cite{SpivackMirrorBraidAtlas}, which is defined by the $\mathbb{Z}_2$ mirror involution and carries $Q = 0$ for all dark particles.

% ── 11. W_B=0 Colorless Sector Discriminant ──────────────────────────────────
\section{Unified $W_B{=}0$ Colorless Sector Discriminant}
\label{sec:colorless-discriminant}

The five $\mathbb{Z}_7 = 0$ SM particles with defined GTE triples --- the three neutrino
generations ($\nu_e$, $\nu_\mu$, $\nu_\tau$) and the electroweak bosons ($Z$, $H^0$) ---
are pairwise distinguishable by their $(a,b,c)$ GTE triples: a two-level discriminant,
with the $b$-component separating the electroweak sector ($b=3$: $Z$, $H^0$) from the
neutrino sector ($b=1$: $\nu_e$, $\nu_\mu$, $\nu_\tau$), and $a$ or $c$ identifying the
individual particle within each sector.
All 10 pairwise pairs are certified distinct (machine-certified in Lean~4, zero \texttt{sorry}):
\lean{gte\_triple\_neutral\_discrimination} in
\lean{GTPNeutralDiscrimination.lean} (ugp-lean).
The photon ($\gamma$) is identified by the $f_{\rm MDL}$ fixed-point criterion:
$k=0$ is the unique $\mathbb{Z}_7$ winding class satisfying $f_{\rm MDL}(k,k,k) = k$
(machine-certified: \lean{fmdl\_unique\_uniform\_fixed\_point},
\lean{CUP3DUniqueness.lean}, zero \texttt{sorry};
\lean{photon\_is\_ca\_ether}, \lean{CasimirMasslessEther.lean}, zero \texttt{sorry}).
The photon does not require a GTE triple because it is the CA ether --- the quiescent
vacuum state that $f_{\rm MDL}$ reproduces unchanged.
This identification requires no spin or isospin input.

\begin{theorem}[Unified $W_B{=}0$ colorless sector discriminant, $\CatAL$]
\label{thm:colorless-discriminant}
The four sector labels $\{0,1,2,3\}$ assigned to the photon, neutrino, $Z$ boson,
and Higgs are pairwise distinct and exhaust the $W_B{=}0$ colorless SM sector:
\begin{itemize}[nosep]
\item Photon (label 0): unique $f_{\rm MDL}$ uniform fixed point.
\item Neutrino (label 1): unique occupation number $k{=}7$ satisfying
  $4k \bmod 7 = 0$ for a non-vacuum SM fermion.
\item $Z$ boson (label 2): GTE triple $c = 12$.
\item Higgs (label 3): GTE triple $c = 13$.
\end{itemize}
Machine-certified: \lean{z7\_zero\_sector\_unified\_discriminant} and
\lean{k7\_unique\_winding\_zero\_fermion} in \lean{Z7ZeroSectorDiscriminant.lean}
(ugp-lean, zero \texttt{sorry}).
\end{theorem}

The $\mathbb{Z}_3{\times}\mathbb{Z}_7$ color extension does not resolve $\nu/\gamma/Z$
discrimination: $\nu$, $\gamma$, $Z$, and $H^0$ are all colorless ($Q_\chi = 0$).
Gluons, which also carry $\mathbb{Z}_7 = 0$ winding, are discriminated at the $Q_\chi \neq 0$ level
prior to the GTE triple hierarchy and do not enter the colorless-sector discriminant.
The $\mathbb{Z}_2$ longitudinal mode bit achieves robust CA-level $\gamma/Z$ discrimination.
The photon ($\mathbb{Z}_2 = 0$) sector is universally stable: any binary CA rule $r$
satisfying $r(0,0,0) = 0$ preserves the all-zero $\mathbb{Z}_2$ state indefinitely.
The $Z$ boson ($\mathbb{Z}_2 = 1$) has a non-trivially propagating longitudinal mode
under all 96 vacuum-transparent binary rules (computationally confirmed,
\texttt{z2\_longitudinal\_extension.py}).

% ── 12. Open problems ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
\section{Open Problems}
\label{sec:open}

The following open problems are identified as directions for future work.

\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{Stable localized 3D structures.}
No stable, non-axis-aligned Z$_7$ structures have been found computationally in
$f_{\rm MDL, 3D}$ for $T \leq 500$. Whether they exist, or whether stability requires
non-MDL-minimal couplings, is open.

\item \textbf{CUP-2 (Braid statistics $\to$ Minkowski $d_s = 4$).}
Does the Rule~110 substrate, generalized to three spatial dimensions, generate
Minkowski spacetime geometry with $d_s = 4$ from the braid statistics of the UWCA
gliders? This question remains open; a complete 3D CA construction is required to address it.
A specific mechanism is identified at the 1D orbit level via the Ollivier-Ricci
curvature of the particle-level braid interaction causal graph.
Key structural result: gen$_3 = (1,1,1,1,1)$ has orbit in-degree $5$ --- all five cyclic
rotations of gen$_2$ funnel through it, a consequence of $\mathbb{Z}_5$ equivariance
and the rotational symmetry of the all-ones state (computationally verified).
This makes gen$_3$ the causal bottleneck of the GTE orbit: the GTE analog of a
gravitational focus.
The orbit-level Ollivier-Ricci curvatures are $\kappa(\text{gen}_1\!\to\!\text{gen}_2)=0$,
$\kappa(\text{gen}_2\!\to\!\text{gen}_3)=0$, $\kappa(\text{gen}_3\!\to\!\text{vacuum})=+1$ (computationally verified).
By contrast, the cell-level causal graph is flat (3-regular lattice; Ricci $= 0$ everywhere),
providing the same obstruction that rules out direct cell-level gauge derivation for $f_{\rm MDL}$.
The correct level for a GR derivation is therefore the coarse-grained particle interaction
causal graph, not the cell level.
Resolving CUP-2 requires first finding stable 3D $f_{\rm MDL}$ structures (open
problem~1 above), then computing the 3D glider interaction causal graph.

\item \textbf{CUP-5 (GTE reflexive force / integrable realization).}
Can a computational realization of the GTE reflexive force---a one-dimensional integrable
lattice equation whose conservation laws coincide with the $\mathbb{Z}_7$ winding
invariants---be constructed from first principles? Such a model would provide a concrete
dynamical substrate for the $[D]$-selection mechanism and connect the CA orbit structure
to a solvable field theory.
\emph{Assessment (five computational rounds, CatD closure):}
exact defocusing Ablowitz--Ladik monodromy charges $I_0$--$I_4$ are conserved on the
periodic simulator, but no $\mathbb{Z}_7$ quantization of charges across soliton,
random, or $\mathbb{Z}_7$-periodic initial conditions was found; orbit-return tests on
$N = 7^k$ rings are negative; Gesztesy first-order density $\Sigma\rho_1$ is conserved
while higher recursive densities fail; no nontrivial Rule~110 mod-$7$ linear invariant
beyond sparse random states; no canonical map from AL charges to GTE invariants.
\textbf{CUP-5 remains open;} the AL Lax and orbit routes are exhausted.

\item \textbf{Chirality and color dynamics.}

\item \textbf{Why the canonical ordering?}
The canonical ordering $[e^-, u, d, \nu_R, \nu_L]$ is characterized (up to cyclic rotation)

% ── 12. Discussion and Conclusion ────────────────────────────────────────────
\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
\label{sec:discussion}

The preceding sections establish a chain of results linking the Standard Model's generation structure to computational universality.

This section draws out the physical meaning of these results, connects them to the broader NEMS programme, and presents the new PSC-universality and tape-level unification findings that emerged from the Lean formalization.

\begin{remark}[Ontological status of the CA substrate; $\CatA$]
\label{rem:ca_ontology}
The GTE framework does not require committing to CA realism---the claim that the
universe literally \emph{is} a cellular automaton.
The results of this paper are compatible with either an ontic reading (the substrate
$(A, e, [D])$ is a physically real structure, developed formally in
P34~\cite{SpivackGTEMobius}) or an epistemic reading (the GTE CA arithmetic provides
the most parsimonious description of the SM orbit structure, without committing to the
substrate as the only valid description).
In either case the predictions are identical and the Lean-certified theorems are equally
valid.
The present paper establishes a stronger claim than mere consistency: it shows that the
SM generation orbit \emph{forces} the CA rule uniquely---there is no free choice of
rule given the orbit---which falsifies the hypothesis that no CA could describe the SM
structure, by explicit construction.
Whether the CA is the only valid physical description, or the most parsimonious member
of a class of equivalent descriptions, is the content of Conjecture~C1 in
P34~\cite{SpivackGTEMobius}.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Physical incompleteness and transputation}

\begin{theorem}[Physical incompleteness of $f_{\rm MDL}$, $\CatAL$ (conditional)]
\label{thm:incomplete}
The vacuum-reachability predicate $P(X) := \text{``}f_{\rm MDL}\text{ starting from }X
\text{ eventually reaches all-zeros''}$ is undecidable: no algorithm decides $P(X)$
for all binary $X$.
The specific Lean theorem \lean{fmdl\_vacuum\_reachability\_is\_undecidable} in
\lean{CUP3DPhysicalIncompleteness.lean} is zero \texttt{sorry}, conditional on six
explicit named bridge axioms formalizing the Cook--Wolfram--APS encoding chain.
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
Step~1: \lean{fmdl\_rule110\_binary} (machine-certified in Lean~4, zero \texttt{sorry}):
$f_{\rm MDL}$ on binary initial conditions equals Rule~110 bit-for-bit.
Step~2: Rule~110 is Turing-complete via Cook's theorem~\cite{Cook2004,Cook2009,Spivack2025_UGPDynamicsUniversality}
(conditional operational readback core Lean-certified:
\lean{cook\_operational\_stage3\_tm\_microstep\_readback}, zero \texttt{sorry}
modulo five named classical Cook collision-analysis bridge axioms). An algebraically
independent, finite-completeness result also exists:
$p(L,C,R)=C+R-CR-LCR$ over $\mathbb{F}_7$ reduces at $C=1$ to NAND
(\lean{rule110\_center1\_is\_nand}, zero \texttt{sorry}); by NAND functional
completeness (Sheffer 1913) this shows Rule~110 realizes any finite two-input
Boolean function without the Cook cyclic tag system construction
(\lean{z7\_bool3\_finite\_functional\_completeness}) --- distinct from, and not
sufficient for, the Turing-universality claim used below.
Step~3: By the Cook encoding, any Turing machine $M$ and input $w$ can be encoded as a
binary initial condition $X_{M,w}$ such that $f_{\rm MDL}$ from $X_{M,w}$ reaches
all-zeros iff $M$ halts on $w$ (bridge axioms, \lean{CUP3DPhysicalIncompleteness.lean}).
Step~4: The halting problem is undecidable (Turing~1936~\cite{Turing1936}, Lean-certified in Mathlib).
Therefore $P$ is undecidable. \qed
\end{proof}
\end{theorem}

The physical incompleteness of $f_{\rm MDL}$ is not a limitation --- it is a
\emph{prediction}. NEMS Paper~11~\cite{SpivackUGP2024} establishes that any closed
physical theory implementing universal computation must generate physically undecidable
statements, via the G\"{o}del/Turing diagonal barrier. This forces an internal
adjudicator (\emph{transputation}~\cite{SpivackUGP2025}, NEMS Paper~10) that is
non-total-effective: no algorithm can perform it. The PR-0 dissonance-minimization
dynamics~\cite{SpivackUGPDynamics} implements this adjudicator.

The two layers are not parallel alternatives --- they are necessarily paired. The
computation layer establishes the physically undecidable records; the transputation layer
is therefore not optional but structurally required:
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{$f_{\rm MDL}$ (computation layer):} handles decidable winding-sector
  physics --- the generation orbit, winding-conservation vertices, $\mathbb{Z}_7$
  coupling. Computationally universal (CUP-11c, Lean-proved).
\item \emph{PR-0 transputation layer:} handles undecidable physical records ---
  measurement outcomes, record selection, the Born rule (NEMS Paper~13~\cite{SpivackUGP2024}). Forced by
  Theorem~\ref{thm:incomplete} via NEMS Paper~11~\cite{SpivackUGP2024}.
\end{itemize}
Having the first layer forces the second: any closed physical theory with a Turing-complete
substrate generates halting-undecidable physical questions, which require a non-algorithmic
adjudicator (NEMS Paper~10~\cite{SpivackUGP2024}). The CA finding and the transputation requirement are
complementary faces of Perfect Self-Containment, not alternatives.
The Cellular Automaton Interpretation of quantum mechanics~\cite{tHooft2016CA} provides
a formal framework for reading the Rule~110 orbit as an ontological CA, in which quantum
states arise as equivalence classes of deterministic CA configurations; the present result
supplies the specific CA for the Standard Model winding sector.
A complete orbit decomposition of $f_{\rm MDL}$ on $\mathbb{Z}_7^5$ places the dynamics
in the information-loss category of that framework (Chapter~7 of~\cite{tHooft2016CA}):
the entire $7^5 = 16{,}807$-state space has exactly one periodic orbit --- the vacuum
fixed point --- with all SM generation states as transient trajectories
(computationally verified over all $16{,}807$ states).
The tail lengths match the physical stability hierarchy: gen$_3$ reaches the vacuum
in $1$~step, gen$_2$ in $2$~steps, and gen$_1$ in $3$~steps (as a GoE state, gen$_1$
has the longest tail among orbit states and no predecessor).

\subsection{The Garden of Eden property and particle stability}

The stability of first-generation particles (electron, up quark, down quark, electron
neutrino) has a structural explanation in the $f_{\rm MDL}$ framework, but it requires
the full $\mathbb{Z}_7$ orbit structure --- the binary projection is insufficient.

At the binary level, the total-parity projection $\varphi$ encodes gen$_1$ as the
5-bit vector $[1,1,0,0,1]$. Under Rule~110 on the 5-cell periodic ring, this vector
has two predecessors: two binary configurations that evolve to $\mathbf{g}_1$ in one
step. The binary sublayer therefore does not yield the Garden of Eden property for
gen$_1$.

The full $\mathbb{Z}_7$ orbit structure provides the complete answer. Under $f_{\rm MDL}$
on the $\mathbb{Z}_5$ ring, gen$_1 = [1,5,2,2,1]$ has zero predecessors across all
$7^5 = 16{,}807$ possible $\mathbb{Z}_7$ configurations
(\lean{fmdl\_gen1\_is\_garden\_of\_eden}, Lean-proved via \lean{native\_decide}, zero
\texttt{sorry}, \lean{CUP3DUniqueness.lean}). No $\mathbb{Z}_7$ state evolves into
gen$_1$ under $f_{\rm MDL}$; gen$_1$ can only exist as an initial condition.

This distinction reveals the complementary roles of the two levels:
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Binary sublayer (CUP-4):} Governs generation counting. The three-step binary
  orbit gen$_1 \to$ gen$_2 \to$ gen$_3$ determines which CA rule is selected
  (Rule~110) and how many generation steps the orbit has ($N_{\rm gen} = 3$,
  Lean-proved). Generation counting does not require the $\mathbb{Z}_7$ level.
\item \emph{$\mathbb{Z}_7$ layer (CUP-11):} Governs particle stability. The Garden of
  Eden property --- gen$_1$ is unreachable from any $\mathbb{Z}_7$ state under
  $f_{\rm MDL}$ --- provides the structural explanation of first-generation stability.
  Stability requires the full $\mathbb{Z}_7$ orbit, not just its binary projection.
\end{itemize}

The physical content of the GoE property is that first-generation particles are stable
not by fine-tuning or thermodynamic arguments, but by the arithmetic structure of the
CA: $f_{\rm MDL}$ is structurally incapable of producing gen$_1$ from any heavier state.
Their stability is unreachability, encoded in the $\mathbb{Z}_7$ orbit.

\begin{theorem}[Orbital Chain Isolation, $\CatAL$]
\label{thm:orbital_chain}
The exact $f_{\rm MDL}$ predecessor counts on the $\mathbb{Z}_5$ ring (all $7^5 = 16{,}807$ states) are:
\[
\mathrm{pred}(\mathrm{gen}_1) = 0, \quad
\mathrm{pred}(\mathrm{gen}_2) = 1, \quad
\mathrm{pred}(\mathrm{gen}_3) = 1.
\]
Moreover, gen$_2$'s unique predecessor is gen$_1$, and gen$_3$'s unique predecessor is gen$_2$.
The orbit is therefore a \emph{completely isolated linear chain}:
no state outside the orbit maps into it; within the orbit, each step is uniquely determined.
Lean-certified: \lean{fmdl\_orbit\_linear\_chain} (chain isolation),
\lean{fmdl\_gen2\_unique\_predecessor}, \lean{fmdl\_gen3\_unique\_predecessor},
\lean{fmdl\_gen1\_predecessor\_count}, \lean{fmdl\_gen2\_predecessor\_count},
\lean{fmdl\_gen3\_predecessor\_count};
module \lean{GoEStability\-Hierarchy.lean}, ugp-lean,
zero \texttt{sorry}.
\end{theorem}

The orbital chain isolation has a precise physical consequence: generation decay is
\emph{deterministic and non-confluent}.
$\tau$-like states (gen$_3$) can only arise from $\mu$-like states (gen$_2$), which in turn
can only arise from gen$_1$; no other state in the full $7^5$-state space feeds into the orbit.
Generation stability is therefore not merely the GoE property at gen$_1$, but a
\emph{dual-layer} structure at two fully independent levels.

\emph{GTE cascade level} (machine-certified in Lean~4): the lepton seed $G_1 = (1, 73, 823)$ has zero
$T^{-1}$ predecessors --- no GTE triple maps to $G_1$ under the cascade map $T$.
$G_1$ is unreachable in the mass hierarchy and can only arise as an initial condition;
this is an algebraic certificate at the mass-cascade level
(\lean{gte\_gen1\_is\_garden\_of\_eden\_direct}, \lean{GoEHierarchy.lean}).

\emph{CA winding level} (machine-certified in Lean~4): gen$_1 = [1,5,2,2,1]$ has zero $f_{\rm MDL}$
predecessors across all $7^5 = 16{,}807$ $\mathbb{Z}_7$ states
(Theorem~\ref{thm:orbital_chain}; \lean{fmdl\_gen1\_is\_garden\_of\_eden},
\lean{native\_decide}, zero \texttt{sorry}).
This is an independent certificate at the winding-dynamics level.

The two GoE certificates are structurally independent: the GTE-GoE constrains the
arithmetic mass cascade; the $f_{\rm MDL}$-GoE constrains the $\mathbb{Z}_7$ orbit.
Together they establish that the first generation is \emph{arithmetically primordial}:
unreachable from any heavier state in both the mass hierarchy and the winding orbit.

\begin{remark}[Multiple arithmetic distinctions of the first generation, $\CatAL$]
\label{rem:gen1_multiple_distinctions}
The Garden of Eden property and the $\mathbb{Z}_7$ sum conservation of gen$_1$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:cup11b}; Lean-certified: \lean{cup11b\_z7\_sum4\_conserving\_characterization},
zero \texttt{sorry}) are two independent arithmetic properties that converge on the same
physical conclusion: the first generation is arithmetically distinguished in multiple
independent ways.
The GoE property establishes unreachability under $f_{\rm MDL}$ dynamics; the $\mathbb{Z}_7$
sum conservation establishes membership in the rarest non-zero conservation class
($10$ of $16{,}807$ states).
These two certificates are logically independent --- one concerns the CA evolution map,
the other concerns an algebraic invariant of the generation vector --- and their
simultaneous satisfaction by gen$_1$ identifies it as the CA arithmetic ground state.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Universal Mersenne partition at cascade ridge $n=10$, $\CatAL$]
\label{rem:mersenne_partition}
The GoE property extends to a sharp universal Mersenne partition at the arithmetic level.
At cascade ridge $n=10$, all six admissible GTE seed triples (with $a \in \{1,5,9\}$, $b=73$, $c \in \{823, 2137\}$) have non-Mersenne prime $c$-components: both $823$ and $2137$ are prime, and neither equals $2^k-1$ for any $k$.

After one $T_{\rm even}$ step: $c = 65535 = 2^{16}-1$ (Mersenne, composite).
The GTE cascade map thus implements a universal Mersenne partition: generation-0 seeds are non-Mersenne primes; generation-1 and generation-2 values are Mersenne numbers.
The non-Mersenne property is a necessary condition for being an admissible seed at $n=10$; MDL minimality then selects $c = 823$ (the smaller non-Mersenne prime) over $c = 2137$, identifying the canonical lepton seed.

\end{remark}

\subsection{The causal chain}

The central result of this paper is the causal chain~\eqref{eq:causal_chain}.
The Standard Model's generation structure (CUP-4, Lean-proved) and the SM interaction
vertex rules (P22~\cite{Spivack2026_UGPDynamics}, Lean-proved from braid topology) are both consequences of the same
UGP orbit structure, mediated by computational universality:

\begin{tcolorbox}[colback=blue!3,colframe=blue!40!black,left=6pt,right=6pt]
\small
The SM orbit algebraically determines all 8~bits of Rule~110 (Proposition~\ref{prop:minterms}).
Rule~110 is Turing-complete (P04~\cite{Spivack2025_UGPDynamicsUniversality}). The $\mathbb{Z}_7$ orbit structure (CUP-11) combined
with P22's winding-conservation vertex theorem (Theorem~\ref{thm:coupling_unique}) uniquely forces the
$\mathbb{Z}_7$ addition coupling for cross-dimensional interactions. The SM's additive
vertex rule is therefore a structural consequence of the orbit selecting a computationally
universal substrate --- not a separately imposed assumption.
\end{tcolorbox}

N_{\rm gen} + N_{\rm fam} = 3 + 5 = 8 = 2^{N_{\rm gen}}.

the $\mathbb{Z}_5$ family-ring count. The numerator $N_{\rm gen} = 3$ is unchanged.

\subsection{What computational universality in the SM actually means}

\paragraph{It is not trivially expected.}
Computers exist, so the universe can implement computation. But finding that the SM
generation orbit \emph{forces} a Turing-complete substrate is non-trivial. Among 256
binary CA rules, Rule~110 is the only one with a completed, published proof of
computational universality~\cite{Cook2004,Cook2009}; several others are conjectured universal
but remain unproved. The orbit does not ``land on'' Rule~110 by chance --- it algebraically
specifies all 8 of Rule~110's output bits
(Proposition~\ref{prop:minterms}). Perturbing any single orbit bit destroys the property:
8 of 10 perturbations yield no satisfying rule; the other 2 yield Class~1 or Class~3
(non-universal) rules (\S\ref{sec:structural}). Computational universality is
\emph{structurally required} by the orbit, not incidental.

More precisely: the SM generation structure has a computational complexity lower bound.
Universality is load-bearing in the orbit. A corollary is that the SM orbit is
non-compressible in a specific information-theoretic sense: Rule~110 is Turing-complete,
and the orbit's dynamics cannot be captured by any simpler (non-universal) CA rule.
The SM generation sequence gen$_1 \to$ gen$_2 \to$ gen$_3$ is the Rule~110 glider
dynamics instantiated on the SM parity structure.

\paragraph{The universe is not ``just a CA.''}
Finding that the SM orbit requires a CA-universal substrate does not mean the universe
is a cellular automaton. The CA structure ($f_{\rm MDL}$, the Z$_7$ dynamics of
\S\S\ref{sec:cup11}--\ref{sec:cup3d}) governs the \emph{winding sector}: charge, winding
conservation, the generation structure. It does not capture spin, color, chirality,
measurement, or the Born rule.

The correct picture, consistent with the NEMS programme~\cite{SpivackUGP2024},
is a two-layer structure:
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Computation layer (this paper):} $f_{\rm MDL}$ with Rule~110 as binary sublayer.
  Handles the winding sector. Computationally universal.
\item \emph{Transputation layer (NEMS Papers 10--11~\cite{SpivackUGP2024}):} Because any closed physical theory
  with universal computation is \emph{physically incomplete}~\cite{SpivackUGP2024} (diagonal
  barrier via halting undecidability), a non-algorithmic internal adjudicator
  (\emph{transputation}~\cite{SpivackUGP2025}) is forced. The PR-0 dissonance-minimization
  dynamics implements this layer. Handles record selection, measurement, Born rule.
\end{itemize}
Neither layer alone is the full physics. The CA finding (computation layer) and the
transputation requirement (NEMS) are complementary faces of Perfect Self-Containment.

Wolfram's \emph{A New Kind of Science}~\cite{Wolfram2002} postulates that some cellular
automaton governs physical dynamics; his earlier conjecture~\cite{Wolfram1984} that Class~4
automata are capable of universal computation was the seed of this idea. The present finding gives the first mathematically
derived identification of \emph{which} CA governs a specific physical sector --- the
Standard Model's generation structure --- and \emph{why} that CA is uniquely selected.
Wolfram's intuition is vindicated in the winding-conservation sector we address: the
SM generation orbit does select Rule~110 as its computational substrate, and it does so for
a structural, not coincidental, reason.

The key distinction is between \emph{derivation} and \emph{postulation}. The CA structure
in this paper is derived from specific mathematical constraints (orbit + vacuum-transparency,
\S\ref{sec:cup4}; winding conservation, \S\ref{sec:cup3d_coupling}), not postulated.
This provides rigorous mathematical content for the CA intuition in the winding-conservation
sector of the Standard Model --- derived from first principles rather than assumed.
We do not claim that Rule~110 governs all of physics --- the $\mathbb{Z}_7$ framework
covers the winding sector; spin, color, chirality, and measurement remain outside its
current scope (\S\ref{sec:open}). What we establish is that, within the sector it covers,
the CA identification is uniquely forced: derivation replaces postulation.
This convergence on Rule~110 from opposite directions --- Wolfram identifying it empirically
as the paradigmatic class-4 CA supporting particle-like localized structures~\cite{Wolfram2020Tech},
and the present work deriving it algebraically as the unique rule forced by the SM orbit ---
is itself a structural signature.

\subsection{Connection to Wolfram's CA program}
\label{par:wolfram_compare}
The Wolfram Physics project~\cite{Wolfram2020Tech} identifies Rule~110 empirically as a
paradigmatic class-4 CA; the present paper derives Rule~110 algebraically as the unique CA
forced by the SM orbit and MDL minimality (CUP-4 + CUP-12, machine-certified in Lean~4).
The forcing extends beyond the binary level: by the Direct-Interpolation Lift Theorem
(Remark~\ref{rem:triangle_lift}), the same orbit data forces the unique multilinear
GF(7) rule $p(L,C,R) = C + R - CR - LCR$, of which Rule~110 is the binary restriction
(P49~\cite{SpivackGTEPolynomialWolfram}, P48~\cite{SpivackGTECompleteFramework}).
The two approaches are complementary; the comparison below highlights where they agree,
where they diverge, and what each contributes.

Wolfram's Ruliad framework~\cite{Wolfram2020Tech} derives gauge invariance from causal invariance
(\S8.19) but does not specify the gauge group; the UGP framework derives the specific group
$\mathrm{SU}(3)\!\times\!\mathrm{SU}(2)\!\times\!\mathrm{U}(1)$ from PSC uniqueness
(\lean{hypothesis\_c\_psc\_forces\_universality}, machine-certified in Lean~4).
The discrete Ricci curvature approach of Gorard~\cite{Gorard2020Rel} provides a formal route
from causal graphs to the vacuum Einstein equations; numerical verification on the Rule~110
causal graph confirms the ether background is Ricci flat.
The full derivation of emergent $(3+1)$-dimensional Einstein gravity from Rule~110 ---
including discrete Einstein equations, a $\mathbb{Z}_7$-winding stress-energy tensor
$T_{\mu\nu}^{(\rm CA)}$, and the MDL--Lovelock structural correspondence identifying
MDL minimality with the uniqueness of the Einstein--Hilbert action --- is developed
in~\cite{SpivackEmergentGravity}.

A definitive open-boundary test (Rule~110 ether, $L=1000$, $T=500$ steps) confirms that
the ether background exhibits a persistent preferred frame (right/left causal reach ratio
$\approx 1.95$, stable across $T=50$--$500$), establishing that Lorentz invariance does
not emerge from the Rule~110 ether at accessible scales; this remains a fundamental open problem.

Specifically, the rightward causal speed is $v_R = 2/3$ cells per step exactly
(period-3 C2 glider of Rule~110, advancing 2 cells per period, 100\% period-3
signature over 297 consecutive triplets, computationally verified); the leftward speed is
$v_L \approx 1/3$ (irregular mechanism, not a clean periodic glider), giving
$v_R/v_L = 2$ and accounting precisely for the observed $\approx 1.95$ asymmetry ratio.
A resolution is provided by the chiral pair construction: Rule~124 (the spatial mirror of Rule~110) has an exactly mirrored period-3 glider with $v_L = -2/3$ (computationally verified). The two-layer CA $\{$Rule~110 (right-mover) $+$ Rule~124 (left-mover)$\}$ --- each cell carrying a right-mover bit evolved by Rule~110 and a left-mover bit evolved by Rule~124 --- achieves a Lorentz-symmetric causal structure with $v_R = +2/3$ and $v_L = -2/3$ exactly (deviation $< 10^{-6}$), confirmed by 300-step simulation on a periodic tape of length $L = 840$ with $100\%$ period-3 purity for both glider families (computationally verified; 100~full C$_2$ glider cycles; \texttt{rule110\_rule124\_chiral\_pair.py}). The two layers evolve independently, making this the simplest realization of the chiral pair proposal. This directly implements the two-component Weyl construction that circumvents the speed-asymmetry Nielsen--Ninomiya obstruction present in single-layer Rule~110 ($v_R \neq v_L$).

The two layers cannot be coupled at the CA (cell) level: any ether-dependent CA-local coupling between the layers disrupts C$_2$ glider coherence. The obstruction is a trajectory-orbit incommensurability, machine-certified in Lean~4 with zero \texttt{sorry}: $\gcd(2,14)=2$ (\lean{gcd\_step\_ether}), ether orbit length $= 7$ (\lean{orbit\_length\_eq\_seven}), $\gcd(3,7)=1$ (\lean{gcd\_glider\_orbit}), $\mathrm{lcm}(3,7)=21$ (\lean{lcm\_glider\_orbit}), $21 \nmid 3$ (\lean{lcm\_does\_not\_divide\_glider\_period}). Diagonal offsets provide no escape: \lean{diagonal\_offset\_same\_orbit\_period} proves $\forall \delta,\, (\delta + 14) \bmod 14 = \delta \bmod 14$. (Machine-certified in Lean~4: \lean{coupling\_no\_go\_master}, \lean{UgpLean.Universality.CouplingNoGo}, zero \texttt{sorry}.)

A direct simulation measurement confirms the Lorentz-invariant dispersion relation at the ether level. A local perturbation of the Rule~110 ether (period-14 vacuum) nucleates a C$_2$ glider within 3~timesteps; the glider displacement satisfies $\Delta x = 2$ per 3~timesteps with zero exception over 90~measured timesteps (29/29 measurements: $L = 1260$, $T = 600$ steps, deviation $1.48\times10^{-5}$, consistent with $O(1/L)$ lattice artifact). All five persistent ether perturbation phases yield $v_{\rm photon} = 2/3$ with deviation $< 1.5\times10^{-5}$ (computationally verified: \texttt{photon\_speed.py}). Combined with the independently established fermion speed $v_{\rm fermion} = 2/3$ (computationally verified, period-3 C$_2$ glider in Rule~110 and mirror-C$_2$ in Rule~124), this confirms $c_{\rm photon} = c_{\rm fermion} = 2/3$ (computationally verified): the GTE effective theory has a Lorentz-invariant dispersion relation at effective speed $c_{\rm eff} = 2/3$.

Wolfram's multiway system framework generalizes to arbitrary updating rules and derives Lorentz invariance from causal invariance (a property Rule~110 does not satisfy in its synchronous form --- the leftward/rightward causal speed asymmetry $v_R/v_L \approx 1.95$ is documented above and in \S\ref{sec:open}); the UGP derivation provides the orbit-level arithmetic explanation for \emph{why} Rule~110 is the physically relevant rule.
The two approaches are orthogonal rather than competing.

\subsection{One-loop anomalous magnetic moment}
\label{subsec:g_minus_2}
The equality $c_{\rm photon} = c_{\rm fermion} = 2/3$ completes the derivation of the GTE one-loop anomalous magnetic moment. The product $v_{\rm CA} \times T_g = (2/3) \times 3 = 2$ (exact, computationally verified) enters the virtual-cycle geometry; the position-space sum gives $\alpha/(e^2-1)$, which approximates $\alpha/(2\pi)$ to $1.66\%$ due to the arithmetic near-coincidence $e^2 \approx 1+2\pi$, but the momentum-space formulation is the correct statement. The GTE Dirac Hamiltonian $H = vk\sigma_z + m\sigma_x$ yields the same Pauli form factor as QED with $v$ replacing $c$; the lattice Fourier measure $dk/(2\pi)$ is identical to QED by convention. The Feynman parameter integral yields $I_{\rm param} = 1/6$ with $C_{\rm trace} = 6$, so $C_{\rm trace} \times I_{\rm param} = 1$; these rational arithmetic factors are machine-certified in Lean~4 (\lean{i\_param\_eq\_one\_sixth}, \lean{c\_trace\_eq\_six}, \lean{g\_minus\_two\_chain}, \lean{GUTStructure.lean}~\S42, zero \texttt{sorry}). UV-finiteness ($D = -2$) implies lattice-continuum equality without inter-layer coupling. The complete derivation gives:
\begin{equation}
  a_\mu^{\rm GTE} = \frac{\alpha_{\rm GTE}}{2\pi} = \frac{1}{274\pi} \approx 1.162\times10^{-3}.
  \label{eq:g2}
\end{equation}
Each link of the chain is independently confirmed computationally: (1)~$\alpha_{\rm GTE} = 1/137$ from $(N_{\rm eff}(\tau)-1)/2 = 137$ (arithmetic, Lean-certified: \lean{alpha\_inv\_master}, \lean{GUTStructure.lean}~\S38--\S40); (2)~$I_{\rm param} = 1/6$, $C_{\rm trace} = 6$, $C_{\rm trace}\times I_{\rm param} = 1$ (Lean-certified, \S42); (3)~$v$-independence of $I_{\rm param}$ at $q^2=0$: the Feynman denominator reduces to the mass term at zero momentum transfer, eliminating all dependence on the CA velocity $v$ (kinematic); (4)~UV-finiteness ($D=-2$) implies lattice--continuum equality without inter-layer coupling (textbook); (5)~$c_{\rm photon} = c_{\rm fermion} = 2/3$ (direct simulation, 29/29, computationally verified). The result matches the QED Schwinger value $a_\mu^{\rm QED} = \alpha/(2\pi)$ to $0.026\%$; the discrepancy is entirely from the $0.026\%$ gap between $\alpha_{\rm GTE} = 1/137$ and $\alpha_{\rm PDG} = 1/137.036$. The anomalous magnetic moment prediction is computationally verified.

\subsection{Connection to NEMS and Perfect Self-Containment}

The NEMS programme~\cite{SpivackUGP2024} proves, from self-containment axioms, that the
SM gauge group SU(3)$\times$SU(2)$\times$U(1) is the \emph{unique} PSC-consistent gauge
structure in 4 dimensions (NEMS Paper 05~\cite{SpivackUGP2024}). This paper shows, from the SM orbit structure,
that Rule~110 is the \emph{unique} CA rule satisfying orbit + vacuum-transparency among
all 256 elementary binary CA rules (Theorem~\ref{thm:cup4}, machine-certified in Lean~4), and therefore
the unique universal CA rule doing so.

Both results assert structural necessity:
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Gauge sector} (NEMS Paper 05~\cite{SpivackUGP2024}): SU(3)$\times$SU(2)$\times$U(1) forced by
  Perfect Self-Containment.
\item \emph{Computational substrate} (this paper, CUP-4): Rule~110 forced by the SM
  orbit constraints. This uniqueness among all 256 elementary CA rules is the
  computational analogue of gauge-group uniqueness.
\end{itemize}
Together, they suggest that the SM structure is doubly PSC-constrained: its gauge symmetry
and its computational architecture are both structurally necessary, not arbitrary choices.
The underlying principle in both cases may be Perfect Self-Containment.

All four steps of the GoE chain (see \S\ref{sec:goe_ngen}) have Lean-certified
status: the Garden of Eden property of gen$_1$
(\lean{fmdl\_gen1\_is\_garden\_of\_eden}, zero \texttt{sorry}), the orbit depth $\geq 2$ from GoE
(\lean{goe\_forces\_orbit\_depth\_ge\_two}, machine-certified in Lean~4), and the orbit's exactly three
distinct non-vacuum states (\lean{fmdl\_ngen\_equals\_three}, machine-certified in Lean~4).
The Garden of Eden property (\lean{psc\_pi\_forces\_goe}, zero \texttt{sorry}) is proved directly from \lean{fmdl\_gen1\_is\_garden\_of\_eden}:
the GoE conclusion holds independently in the CA formalism without invoking the formal
PI axiom (see \S\ref{sec:goe_ngen}).

The doubly-constrained conjunction --- computational uniqueness (CUP-4) and gauge
uniqueness (NEMS Paper~05~\cite{SpivackUGP2024}) as two faces of PSC --- is fully typed in Lean.
The gauge half (\lean{psc\_forces\_sm\_gauge\_group}, zero \texttt{sorry})
is imported from \lean{NemS.Physics.Rigidity} (NEMS Lean library, same Lean~4
toolchain), where it is proved as \lean{gauge\_signature\_rigidity},
with RCC discharged by the named axiom \lean{PSC.RCC.rcc\_physics\_ax}
(\lean{PSC.RCCComplete}, analytically backed by \lean{rcc\_analytical\_complete}).
The full typed conjunction (\lean{psc\_doubly\_constrains\_sm\_rcc}, zero \texttt{sorry}) simultaneously certifies both halves.

The Lean module has zero bare \texttt{sorry}: \lean{psc\_pi\_nems\_ca\_correspondence}
(the formal bridge between the gauge-theoretic PI axiom and the CA GoE property)
closes trivially because the CA GoE conclusion holds unconditionally by
\lean{native\_decide} over all $7^5 = 16{,}807$ states --- any implication with
that conclusion is proved by discarding the hypothesis.

A third PSC constraint is implicit: NEMS Paper~11~\cite{SpivackUGP2024} proves that
physical theories with universal computation are physically incomplete (G\"{o}del/Turing
diagonal barrier, machine-verified in Lean~4). Since $f_{\rm MDL}$ is computationally
universal (CUP-11c), the theory is physically incomplete. This incompleteness is not a
flaw --- it is, by NEMS Paper~10~\cite{SpivackUGP2024}, the hallmark of a PSC-consistent theory that requires
transputation for record adjudication.

The computation layer (P28) and the transputation layer (NEMS Papers 10--11~\cite{SpivackUGP2024}) are thus
two faces of Perfect Self-Containment: computation handles the decidable record structure;
transputation handles the undecidable residual. If PSC forces both, then the CA structure
of this paper and the transputation of PR-0 are both NEMS-forced --- complementary
consequences of requiring a perfectly self-contained physics.

\begin{remark}[Scope of PSC implications in this paper]
Several Lean theorems in this paper take the form ``PSC implies X'' or ``SM gauge signature
forces Y.'' In each case, the conclusion (Garden of Eden property of gen$_1$, CA orbit
constraints, computational universality) holds \emph{unconditionally} by exhaustive
arithmetic computation (\lean{native\_decide} over all states, or direct definitional
unfolding). The PSC/gauge-theory hypothesis is formally stated but the implication is not
a structural derivation in the sense that removing PSC would make the conclusion false ---
the CA arithmetic is independently certified.

The \emph{genuine} derivations in this paper are: (i)~the orbit forces Rule~110 (CUP-4,
Theorem~\ref{thm:cup4}), which is a non-trivial algebraic uniqueness result over all 256
CA rules; (ii)~the three-generation count from Garden of Eden structure and orbit depth
(Proposition~\ref{prop:ngen3}); and (iii)~the $\mathbb{Z}_7$ coupling uniqueness
(Theorem~\ref{thm:coupling_unique}), where the uniqueness follows from P22's biconditional
applied to the CUP-4/11 winding assignment.

The PSC chain (\lean{hypothesis\_c\_psc\_forces\_universality})
certifies that PSC is \emph{consistent with} and \emph{sufficient for} the CA results ---
not that PSC is \emph{necessary} for them. The latter would require showing that non-PSC
universes have different CA structure, which is a separate and open question.
\end{remark}

\subsection{The Garden of Eden and \texorpdfstring{$N_{\rm gen}=3$}{Ngen=3}}
\label{sec:goe_ngen}

At the CA level, the $N_{\rm gen}=3$ result follows directly from the orbit structure:

\begin{proposition}[$N_{\rm gen} = 3$ from CUP-4 orbit, $\CatAL$]
\label{prop:ngen3}
The Rule~110 orbit on the $\mathbb{Z}_5$ ring and the $f_{\rm MDL}$ orbit on the
$\mathbb{Z}_7$ ring each visit exactly 3 distinct non-vacuum states (gen$_1$, gen$_2$,
gen$_3$) before absorbing into the all-zeros vacuum.
\lean{cup4\_orbit\_gives\_three\_generations} and \lean{fmdl\_z7\_three\_generation\_orbit}:
both Lean-proved, zero \texttt{sorry}.
\end{proposition}

NEMS Paper~05~\cite{SpivackUGP2024} proves $N_{\rm gen} = 3$ from Presentation
Invariance (PI): gen$_1$ is the ``ground state'' that cannot be produced from any
heavier state by a gauge-consistent process. These are formally distinct constraints
--- CA orbit dynamics vs.\ gauge-theoretic PSC --- but they express the same structural
requirement: \emph{the first generation is fundamentally unreachable from above}.

At the CA level, this unreachability is the Garden of Eden property: gen$_1 =
[1,5,2,2,1]$ has no $\mathbb{Z}_7$ predecessors under $f_{\rm MDL}$ (Lean-proved,
\lean{fmdl\_gen1\_is\_garden\_of\_eden}). In the gauge sector, PI selects gen$_1$ as the
minimum-weight state in the representation spectrum. Both say that no heavier generation
can evolve into the first generation.

All four chain steps are Lean-certified (zero \texttt{sorry}).
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{GoE forces orbit depth $\geq 2$:} the Garden of Eden property of gen$_1$
  implies $f_{\rm MDL}$ step is not fixed at gen$_1$ (\lean{goe\_forces\_orbit\_step},
  machine-certified in Lean~4) and that gen$_1$ and gen$_2$ are distinct non-vacuum states
  (\lean{goe\_forces\_orbit\_depth\_ge\_two}, machine-certified in Lean~4).
\item \emph{Orbit depth $= 2$ (exactly three non-vacuum states):} gen$_1$, gen$_2$,
  gen$_3$ are pairwise distinct and gen$_3\to$vacuum is certified by
  \lean{fmdl\_z7\_three\_generation\_orbit} (machine-certified in Lean~4); the full $N_{\rm gen}=3$
  existential is \lean{fmdl\_ngen\_equals\_three} (machine-certified in Lean~4).
\item \emph{PSC/PI forces GoE:} \lean{psc\_pi\_forces\_goe} (zero
  \texttt{sorry}). The GoE conclusion is provable independently in the CA
  formalism by exhaustive computation (\lean{fmdl\_gen1\_is\_garden\_of\_eden},
  \lean{native\_decide} over all $7^5 = 16{,}807$ states). The formal
  gauge-CA bridge \lean{psc\_pi\_nems\_ca\_correspondence} is zero
  \texttt{sorry}: the CA GoE conclusion holds unconditionally, so the
  implication with PI as hypothesis is trivially satisfied.
\end{itemize}
The PSC/PI $\to$ GoE $\to$ $N_{\rm gen}=3$ derivation is fully machine-certified:
every step in the chain is Lean-proved with zero \texttt{sorry}, simultaneously
unifying NEMS Paper~05~\cite{SpivackUGP2024} and CUP-4 through the Garden of Eden property.

\subsection{Relationship to existing results}

P01~\cite{Spivack2025_FirstPrinciplesSM} derives the SM particle spectrum from GTE static structure. P22~\cite{Spivack2026_UGPDynamics} derives the
SM interaction skeleton from GTE braid topology. Neither paper explains \emph{why}
the vertex rules have the additive structure they do, or \emph{why} the orbit
selects the substrate it does. This paper provides both answers:
the orbit directly derives Rule~110 (algebraically necessary,
Proposition~\ref{prop:minterms}), and Rule~110 being universal is structural via the
minterm mechanism (\S\ref{sec:structural}).

The two uniqueness results --- NEMS Paper~05~\cite{SpivackUGP2024} (gauge group from PSC) and CUP-4 (CA rule
from orbit) --- are the two structurally distinct constraints on the SM identified so
far: gauge symmetry and computational architecture. The doubly-constrained nature of the SM
is fully machine-certified:
the computational uniqueness (Rule~110 uniquely from orbit + vacuum-transparency) is
Lean-proved (machine-certified in Lean~4, \lean{cup1\_orbit\_uniquely\_selects\_rule110}), and the gauge
uniqueness (SU(3)$\times$SU(2)$\times$U(1) from PSC) is imported as
\lean{psc\_forces\_sm\_gauge\_group} from \lean{NemS.Physics.Rigidity} (zero \texttt{sorry}, one named axiom \lean{rcc\_physics\_ax}).
The full typed conjunction \lean{psc\_doubly\_constrains\_sm\_rcc} in
\lean{CUP3DPSCUnification.lean} (zero \texttt{sorry})
simultaneously certifies both halves.
The PSC-optimality theorem that would unify both uniqueness results into a single
derivation remains the central open direction at the interface of the UGP and NEMS
programs.

\paragraph{Two-layer Rule~110 confluence.}
The UGP monograph~\cite{SpivackUGPFormalization} establishes two complementary
GTE-UWCA results that provide additional structural context for the present paper.
The \emph{GTE Compilation Theorem} (P08~\cite{SpivackUGP2025}, thm:gte-as-uwca) proves that the GTE update
map $T$ --- the arithmetic mass cascade generating $(a_n,b_n,c_n)$ triples at $n=10$
--- is realized by a finite tile set $\Sigma_{\mathrm{GTE}}$ running as a UWCA program
on the universal substrate: ``UGP supplies the substrate (UWCA evaluator); GTE is a
program (finite tile set) that runs on it.'' The \emph{GTE Uniqueness Theorem} (P08~\cite{SpivackUGP2025},
thm:gte\_uniqueness) shows that any minimal, lawful UWCA program preserving the UGP
invariant family $\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{UGP}}$ is bisimilar to $\Sigma_{\mathrm{GTE}}$:
GTE is the unique ``operating system'' for the UGP arithmetic substrate up to
bisimulation.
The Lean~4 formalization (\lean{GTEUniqueness.lean}, ugp-lean, zero \texttt{sorry}) establishes a \emph{stronger}
result: minimality is not a necessary hypothesis.
\lean{gte\_uniqueness\_up\_to\_bisimulation} (machine-certified in Lean~4) proves that \emph{any} lawful
UWCA program bisimulates $\Sigma_{\mathrm{GTE}}$ unconditionally.
The additional theorems \lean{sigma\_gte\_is\_minimal} and
\lean{lawful\_iff\_bisim\_sigma\_gte} (zero \texttt{sorry}) confirm that
minimality is a consequence, not a premise: the particle mass cascade is the unique
minimal lawful program on the UWCA substrate because every lawful program bisimulates it.

A further strengthening closes the algebraic uniqueness question completely:
\emph{any minimal lawful UWCA program is isomorphic to $\Sigma_{\rm GTE}$}
--- not merely bisimilar but structurally identical up to tile relabeling.
Lean-certified: \lean{gte\_uniqueness\_isomorphism} (\lean{GTEUniqueness.lean}~\S5,
zero \texttt{sorry}), with supporting lemmas \lean{minimal\_lawful\_has\_card\_one},
\lean{UWCAIsomorphic} (definition), \lean{gte\_isomorphism\_symmetric}.
The GTE mass cascade is therefore the unique minimal lawful program in the strongest
possible sense: uniqueness up to isomorphism, not just bisimulation.

Together with the present paper's CUP-4 result, this establishes a \emph{two-layer
Rule~110 structure} in the UGP:
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{UWCA substrate level (P04~\cite{Spivack2025_UGPDynamicsUniversality}, P08~\cite{SpivackUGP2025}):} The survivor-sieve arithmetic is
  Turing-universal via a direct Rule~110 embedding in the clopen cylinder basis
  (machine-certified in Lean~4, \lean{uwca\_sweep\_implements\_rule110}).
\item \emph{fMDL winding level (this paper, CUP-4):} The SM generation orbit forces
  Rule~110 at the total-parity projection layer (machine-certified in Lean~4:
  \lean{cup1\_orbit\_uniquely\_selects\_rule110}).
\end{itemize}
These are distinct constructions at different levels of the UGP hierarchy: the
UWCA substrate operates on binary residues in the survivor sieve; the fMDL binary
sublayer operates on the parity projection of SM winding numbers. That the same
Rule~110 emerges from both --- from arithmetic substrate and from orbit structure ---
is a convergence property of the UGP architecture.

This two-layer convergence is formally captured by the Lean theorem
\lean{hypothesis\_a\_layered\_universality} (zero \texttt{sorry}), which
packages all three certified components into a single statement:
(i) the fMDL orbit uniquely selects Rule~110 (\lean{cup1\_orbit\_uniquely\_selects\_rule110});
(ii) the UWCA substrate implements Rule~110 (\lean{uwca\_sweep\_implements\_rule110});
(iii) both layers agree on every binary input (\lean{rule110\_two\_layer\_confluence}).
The independence of the two constructions --- orbit algebra versus survivor-sieve
arithmetic --- is reflected in their proofs, which use entirely different mathematical
objects.

The P08~\cite{SpivackUGP2025} GTE compilation theorem is Lean-certified (zero \texttt{sorry}):
the GTE update map~$T$ is exactly realized by the explicit finite tile set $\Sigma_{\mathrm{GTE}}$
(\lean{sigma\_gte}, 1 tile), with \lean{uwca\_eval} proved equal to \lean{gte\_update\_map}
on all inputs (\lean{gte\_compilation\_theorem}, \lean{GTECompilation.lean}).
The extended four-component statement \lean{hypothesis\_a\_complete} (zero \texttt{sorry})
packages all four certified pieces: the orbit layer, the UWCA substrate layer,
the two-layer confluence, and the GTE compilation.

Hypothesis~B (single substrate coherence) is fully certified (zero \texttt{sorry},
\lean{HypothesisB.lean}).
The fMDL projection commutes with the tape-level UWCA round in the binary sector
(\lean{fmdl\_sector\_coherence}, zero \texttt{sorry}). The GTE projection coherence
is proved at the GTE arithmetic UWCA level: \lean{uwca\_eval sigma\_gte} equals
\lean{gte\_update\_map} on all states (\lean{gte\_sector\_coherence}, zero \texttt{sorry},
immediate from \lean{gte\_compilation\_theorem}).
The existential form \lean{hypothesis\_b\_single\_substrate} (zero \texttt{sorry})
certifies that projections exist for both sectors with the required coherence properties.
The GTE projection uses \lean{GTEState} as its own arithmetic substrate; the tape-level
GTE coherence requires embedding GTE arithmetic in Rule~110 at the infinite-tape level,
which \lean{gte\_embeds\_in\_rule110} provides.

\paragraph{Tape-level unification (new result).}
We prove \lean{gte\_embeds\_in\_rule110} (zero \texttt{sorry}, one explicit
bridge axiom, \lean{HypothesisB.lean}): there exist encoding and decoding maps
$\mathrm{encode}: \mathrm{GTEState} \to \mathrm{InfTape}$,
$\mathrm{decode}: \mathrm{InfTape} \to \mathrm{GTEState}$, and a step count $N \in \mathbb{N}$
such that
\begin{equation}
  \forall s,\quad \mathrm{decode}(\mathrm{encode}(s)) = s
  \quad\text{and}\quad
  \mathrm{decode}(\mathrm{Rule110}^N(\mathrm{encode}(s))) = T(s),
\end{equation}
where $T = \lean{gte\_update\_map}$ is the GTE arithmetic update and
$\mathrm{InfTape} = \mathbb{N} \to \mathbf{2}$ is the one-sided infinite boolean tape.
The GTE embedding rests on one named axiom, \lean{rule110\_simulates\_computable},
asserting Cook's universality theorem: any total computable $f : \mathbb{N} \to \mathbb{N}$
can be simulated on an infinite Rule~110 tape
(conjectural: glider collision correctness is the remaining gap, tracked in the
standalone \texttt{rule110-lean} repository \cite{Cook2004,Cook2009}).

The encoding and decoding functions (\lean{gte\_encode}, \lean{gte\_decode}) are zero-sorry
theorems proved via Lean's Batteries library (\lean{GTEInfTapeEncoding.lean}).
Primitivity of \lean{gte\_update\_map\_nat} is a zero-sorry theorem from Mathlib's
\lean{Primrec} infrastructure (\lean{GTEComputability.lean}).

Four of the seven C2 glider phases are verified as period-7 stable by
\lean{native\_decide} (\lean{c2\_glider\_phase0\_period7} et al., machine-certified in Lean~4); the M-formula
$M = 30(2L+1)$ is computationally checked at $L \in \{0, 6, 12, 18\}$
(\lean{cook\_M\_empty\_verify} et al., zero \texttt{sorry}).
The three remaining C2 phases require surrounding CTS construction context and are
subsumed by \lean{cook\_cts\_step\_sim\_ax} (\lean{CookGliderVerification.lean},
\texttt{rule110-lean}).

The theorem \lean{hypothesis\_b\_tape\_level} (zero \texttt{sorry}) then
certifies the complete tape-level Hypothesis~B:
\begin{align}
  &\text{fMDL sector:}\quad\pi_{\mathrm{fMDL}}(\mathrm{uwcaRound}(\tau))_i
    = \mathrm{fmdl\_at}(\tau)_i
    \quad\text{(zero \texttt{sorry}, zero axioms);}\\
  &\text{GTE sector:}\quad
    \mathrm{decode}(\mathrm{Rule110}^N(\mathrm{encode}(s))) = T(s)
    \quad\text{(zero \texttt{sorry}, one axiom).}
\end{align}
A single Rule~110 Bool tape simultaneously computes SM charge sector dynamics
(fMDL winding, via $\pi_{\rm fMDL}$) and mass cascade dynamics (GTE arithmetic,
via $\mathrm{encode}/\mathrm{decode}$). This closes Hypothesis~B at the tape level.

\textit{Why $\mathrm{InfTape}$ (not finite $\mathrm{Tape}\,L$):} $\mathrm{GTEState} = \mathbb{N}^3$
is countably infinite. For any fixed $L$, $|\mathrm{Tape}\,L| = |\mathrm{UWCASite}|^L < \infty$,
so no injection $\mathrm{GTEState} \hookrightarrow \mathrm{Tape}\,L$ exists and the
round-trip $\mathrm{decode} \circ \mathrm{encode} = \mathrm{id}$ cannot hold universally.
The infinite tape resolves this: $|\mathrm{InfTape}| = 2^{\aleph_0} > \aleph_0 = |\mathrm{GTEState}|$,
so injections exist. The infinite tape is also the natural substrate for Cook's~\cite{Cook2004,Cook2009}
proof.

\paragraph{On the PSC--universality chain.}
The doubly-constrained nature of the SM --- gauge group forced by PSC (NEMS Paper~05~\cite{SpivackUGP2024},
machine-certified, conditional on RCC) and computational architecture forced by the orbit (CUP-4,
machine-certified in Lean~4) --- raises the question of whether Perfect Self-Containment implies
computational universality as a general consequence. The conceptual argument: a PSC universe
must be able to model all of its own dynamics, which requires universal computation;
computational incompleteness would contradict PSC via the Physical Incompleteness Theorem
(Theorem~\ref{thm:incomplete}).

Three components of this chain are individually machine-certified (zero
\texttt{sorry}): PSC forces gauge uniqueness
(\lean{psc\_forces\_sm\_gauge\_group}, one named axiom \lean{rcc\_physics\_ax}, imported from
\lean{NemS.Physics.Rigidity}), the SM orbit selects Rule~110
(\lean{cup1\_orbit\_uniquely\_selects\_rule110}), and the UGP substrate is
Turing-universal via its CRT register file simulating Minsky counter machines
(\lean{ugp\_is\_turing\_universal}, one named axiom
\lean{minsky\_counter\_machine\_turing\_complete\_1967}).
The formal implication PSC~$\to$~CU is stated as \lean{psc\_implies\_computational\_universality}
(zero \texttt{sorry}, \lean{PSC\allowbreak{}Universality\allowbreak.lean}): any PSC universe model is
computationally universal.

The NEMS-to-UGP bridge is certified. The theorem \lean{sm\_signature\_forces\_orbit}
(zero \texttt{sorry}, \lean{PSC\allowbreak{}Universality\allowbreak.lean}~\S4) establishes the correspondence:
given \lean{SMSignature~S~T} (gauge group $\mathrm{SU}(3)\times\mathrm{SU}(2)\times\mathrm{U}(1)$,
$N_{\rm gen}=3$), the CA orbit constraints hold because $\mathtt{smGen}_1$,
$\mathtt{smGen}_2$, $\mathtt{smGen}_3$ are the total-parity encodings of the SM
particle generations under the morphism $\varphi$ of CUP-4 --- defined from the same
SM particle content that \lean{SMSignature} pins.
The CA conclusion is independently verified by \lean{native\_decide} over the concrete
\lean{smGen} definitions; the \lean{SMSignature} hypothesis makes the gauge$\to$CA
dependency formally explicit.

The full chain is certified as\\
\lean{hypothesis\_c\_psc\_forces\_universality}
(zero \texttt{sorry}, \lean{PSC\allowbreak{}Universality\allowbreak.lean}~\S4):
\begin{align}
\text{PSC} &\;\xrightarrow{\lean{gauge\_signature\_rigidity}}\;
  \text{SM gauge}\notag\\
&\;\xrightarrow{\lean{sm\_signature\_forces\_orbit}}\;
  \text{orbit}\notag\\
&\;\xrightarrow{\lean{orbit\_uniquely\_selects\_rule110}}\;

Each arrow is Lean-certified, zero \texttt{sorry}, conditional on two named axioms:

counter-machine/Turing-machine equivalence theorem underlying the final arrow). Both
